The patch's code is not at fault. The Python suite (2,661 passed) and freestanding (36) are green, and the 18 compiler-test failures are one whitespace problem.\n\n**What fails.** Every failure is the last record of a golden file:\n- `sawc2 lex` prints `EOF\\t6:1\\t`, with a trailing tab for the empty text field;\n- the server's copy of the fixture ends `EOF\\t6:1`, with no tab.\n\nThe `--docs` case is the same thing for a doc record with empty text (`DOC\\t8:1\\tdoc\\t`).\n\n**Why.** The submitted diff has the tab (`+EOF\\t6:1\\t`). The server applies patches with `git apply --3way --index` (sawtracker `store.saw`, `apply_in_worktree`) and no `--whitespace` flag, so git's config decides. The global git config on the Air has `apply.whitespace = fix`, and very likely the Studio's does too: same user, same setup. I reproduced it locally:\n- `git apply --3way --index` of this exact diff onto origin/main strips the tab, and `comments.tokens` ends `EOF\\t6:1\\n`;\n- the same diff applied with `--whitespace=nowarn` keeps it, and `compiler/tests/run.py` is then green. My review run gave `compiler tests: ok`, and the battery's `compiler`, `minivm` (446 cases) and `citations` lanes all pass.\n\n**Fixes.**\n1. **In this patch (suggested):** make the canonical dump never end a record in whitespace, and regenerate the affected expectations. A record whose last field is empty (EOF's text, an empty doc line) should omit the trailing separator, or the empty field should be escaped. A fixture format with significant trailing whitespace is fragile in general: editors that trim on save, `git apply --whitespace=fix`, and review tools all remove it silently.\n2. **In sawtracker (worth an ST issue):** apply with an explicit `--whitespace=nowarn`, so the tested and merged tree is byte-for-byte the reviewed diff. Today, any added line ending in whitespace is merged differently from how it was reviewed. That includes Markdown hard line breaks (two trailing spaces), and expected-output lines with trailing spaces.\n\nThe rest of my review (the gate, the runner, the driver, the lexer move, the battery, CI and docs) follows once the subset checker's deep-dive finishes.\n","created":"1790346821","id":"c3","kind":"review"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Agreed; same diagnosis. This machine's global git config sets apply.whitespace = fix, so the server's git apply strips the golden records' trailing tab. The fix in progress on the branch: the dump format never ends a record in whitespace (an empty last field is omitted with its tab, e.g. EOF\\t6:1), the goldens are regenerated, and the runner refuses any golden line ending in whitespace, naming the file and line. A revision follows.","created":"1790346829","id":"c4"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Revised patch SL-399.p1 to revision 2","created":"1790347094","id":"c5"}],"created":"1790342966","description":"Unit 1 of the self-hosted compiler epic. Read the epic, SL:architecture (§3.0, §3.1 and §4) and SL:hazards first.\n\n## Goal\n\nThe `compiler/` directory exists and builds with the frozen compiler (Stage 0). It holds the lexer, with a driver binary that dumps tokens, golden token fixtures, a test runner, and the subset checker. `./build.sh test` is path-aware. The syntax diff lanes that the golden fixtures replace are retired.\n\n## Rulings this unit implements (user, Sep 25)\n\n- `compiler/` at the top level, one directory per stage.\n- The lexer moves in from `selfhost/lexer`. `prototypes/minivm` stays where it is, paused.\n- Internal tests until self-hosting: golden dumps, plus small subset programs the frozen compiler builds.\n- The per-patch gate is path-aware.\n- The syntax diff lanes retire into a one-time golden snapshot.\n\n## Work\n\n### A. Layout and build\n- `git mv selfhost/lexer compiler/lex`, so its history follows. It stays a package the other stages import through `--module-path`. Keep its package name unless a rename is clearly better, and say which you chose. Re-point `prototypes/minivm`'s `--module-path sawlex=selfhost/lexer` (its README and the battery `minivm` lane) so it still builds.\n- `compiler/driver/`: the compiler binary, named `sawc2` for now (provisional). Its first subcommand is `sawc2 lex [--docs] <file>`, which emits the lexer's existing canonical dump format. It absorbs `sawlex`'s CLI, so there is one entry point, not two.\n- One build entry (a small script, or a `build.sh` subcommand) that builds `sawc2` with the frozen compiler and the right `--module-path` flags. Output goes to `.build/`.\n\n### B. Tests\n- **Unit programs:** the lexer's `tests/*.saw` move with it. Each is built by the frozen compiler and passes by exiting 0.\n- **Golden token fixtures** in `compiler/tests/lex/`: an input `.saw`, its expected `.tokens`, and a `.docs` expectation where doc trivia matter.\n  - Choose the fixture set so that every token kind and every lex-error kind appears at least once. Take the fixtures from existing tracked `.saw` files and the lexer tests' inputs.\n  - Snapshot procedure: generate each expectation with `sawc2 lex`, confirm it is byte-identical to the Python lexer's dump (the machinery `tools/lexdiff.py` uses), then commit.\n  - Before retiring lexdiff, run its full sweep over every tracked `.saw` once, and record the result (it should be zero mismatches) in your report.\n- **Coverage check:** the runner fails if some token kind appears in no fixture. Get the list of kinds from the lexer itself (for example a `sawc2 lex --kinds` subcommand), not from a hand-kept copy.\n- **The runner**, `compiler/tests/run.py` (Python is fine for now):\n  - it builds `sawc2` and the unit programs with the frozen compiler;\n  - it runs the unit programs, the golden fixtures and the subset checker;\n  - its output is deterministic, it prints one summary line, and it exits nonzero on any failure.\n\n### C. The subset checker\n- A tool under `compiler/` that refuses subset violations in the compiler's own source. It covers every `compiler/**/*.saw` except test fixtures (`compiler/tests/**`, which hold arbitrary Saw) and `*.test.saw`.\n- Implement the syntactic rules from SL:architecture §4's blanket list, plus the SL:hazards entries whose Checker line says `yes` and whose check is cheap. Add an **import allowlist**: the std modules the compiler may import, starting with what the lexer and driver need. The allowlist keeps the compiler's std cone small for the bootstrap std unit.\n- Parsing the source with the frozen compiler's own front end (for example `--emit-ast`) is fine, and it is more robust than regexes. Each diagnostic gives `file:line`, the rule's name, and the SL:hazards entry or §4 rule it enforces.\n- **Self-test:** a fixture per rule that the checker must refuse, and a clean fixture it must accept.\n- If the moved lexer violates a rule, bring the lexer into the subset. List every change in your report. Never weaken a rule to fit the code.\n\n### D. The path-aware gate\n- `.sawtracker/tests.json` runs `./build.sh test --changed-since HEAD^`. The server tests the applied commit, whose parent is the patch's base. Without the flag, `./build.sh test` runs everything.\n- It always builds and runs the compiler tests. It runs the Python suite (`test_runner.py`) and `tools/freestanding_runner.py` only when a changed path is one those suites depend on. Derive that set from the two runners (for example `sawc/`, `examples/`, `libs/`, the runners themselves, `build.sh` and `.sawtracker/`), and list it with a comment saying why. If the changed paths cannot be determined, run everything.\n- Keep the suite lock around everything the gate runs. Print which parts ran and why.\n- Add a dry-run flag that prints the decision for a given diff. Use it to show a compiler-only diff skipping the Python suite, and a `sawc/` diff running it.\n\n### E. Battery\n- Remove the `lexdiff` and `selfhostlex` lanes. The compiler tests replace them.\n- Add a `compiler` lane that runs `compiler/tests/run.py`, and keep `minivm` building from the new path.\n- Delete `tools/lexdiff.py` after the snapshot. Delete `tools/dump_tokens.py` too, unless something else still uses it; check `astdiff` and the rest of `tools/`.\n\n### F. Docs\n- `compiler/README.md`: the layout, how to build and test, the subset and its checker, and pointers to the SL docs.\n- `CLAUDE.md`: the repo map entry, the gate policy, and the retired lanes.\n- `TESTING.md`: the compiler tests.\n- Follow CLAUDE.md's rules for code comments.\n\n## Gates (standing ruling: targeted only; the server runs the suite and freestanding)\n- `compiler/tests/run.py` is green.\n- The touched battery lanes are green: `compiler`, `minivm`, and `citations` if `tools/` changed.\n- The gate's dry-run output shows both decisions.\n- Do **not** run the full suite or freestanding.\n\n## Out of scope\nThe parser, any new syntax, golden AST fixtures and std changes.\n\n## Report\n- the files moved and created;\n- the package and module layout you chose;\n- the checker's rules, with the source of each;\n- fixture coverage;\n- the snapshot verification and lexdiff sweep results;\n- every change made to bring the lexer into the subset;\n- the gate's dry-run output;\n- anything that needs the lead or the user.\n","id":"SL-399","labels":["compiler"],"order":1,"parent":"SL-398","priority":"normal","project":"SL","queue_order":0,"revision":6,"sequence":2475,"stage":"queued","status":"open","title":"U1: compiler/ skeleton, the lexer moved in, golden token fixtures, the subset checker, the path-aware Print which parts ran and why.\n- Add a dry-run flag that prints the decision for a given diff. Use it to show a compiler-only diff skipping the Python suite, and a `sawc/` diff running it.\n\n### E. Battery\n- Remove the `lexdiff` and `selfhostlex` lanes. in short, the runner:\\n+\\n+- builds `sawc2` and every unit program (`compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`) with the\\n+  frozen compiler, and runs each unit program: exit 0 is a pass;\\n+- compares `sawc2 lex` with each golden fixture in `compiler/tests/lex/`\\n+  (`NAME.saw` beside its expected `NAME.tokens`, and `NAME.docs` for doc\\n+  trivia), byte for byte;\\n+- refuses a fixture line that ends in whitespace, or a fixture that ends in a\\n+  blank line, naming the file and line: the patch server applies patches with\\n+  `git apply --whitespace=fix`, which strips both;\\n+- fails if a token kind the lexer declares, or a kind of lex error it reports,\\n+  appears in no fixture;\\n+- runs the subset checker (`compiler/tools/subset_check.py`) over the compiler's\\n+  own source, and over its fixtures in `compiler/tests/subset/`, whose\\n+  `// refuses: RULE` markers name exactly what each must report.\\n+\\n+It prints one line per failure, then one summary line, and exits 1 on any\\n+failure. The golden fixtures replaced the lexer differential (`lexdiff`): they\\n+were checked once against the Python lexer, and they are the lexer's oracle now.\\n+\\n+### The per-patch gate\\n+\\n+`./build.sh test` is what sawtracker runs on every submitted patch, as\\n+`./build.sh test --changed-since HEAD^` (`.sawtracker/tests.json`). The\\n+decision is `tools/patch_gate.py`'s:\\n+\\n+- the compiler tests above always run;\\n+- the Python suite runs when a changed path is one of `test_runner.py`'s inputs\\n+  (`sawc/`, `examples/`, the vector files under `tests/` the examples read, the\\n+  runner itself);\\n+- the freestanding suite runs when a changed path is one of its runner's inputs\\n+  (`sawc/`, `tests/freestanding/`, `blade/`, `libs/`, the runner itself);\\n+- a change to the gate itself (`build.sh`, `tools/patch_gate.py`,\\n+  `.sawtracker/`) runs everything, and so does a run whose changed paths are\\n+  unknown.\\n+\\n+It prints each part's decision with its reason. `--dry-run` prints the decision\\n+and runs nothing, for a revision (`--changed-since REV`) or a patch file\\n+(`--diff FILE`), which is how to see what a squashed patch will cost before\\n+submitting it:\\n+\\n+```bash\\n+./build.sh test --dry-run --diff /tmp/patch.diff\\n+```\\n+\\n+`build.sh` holds the suite lock around everything the gate runs. From a\\n+worktree, point `SAW_PYTHON` at the main checkout's venv rather than letting it\\n+bootstrap one.\\n+\\n ## The Gate Battery\\n \\n ```bash\\n@@ -548,17 +605,13 @@ a stand-in `$PATH`, a pre-populated fetch cache — so no step depends on what\\n happens to be installed on the machine, and the fetch step is exercised without\\n touching the network.\\n \\n-### The selfhostlex stage\\n+### The compiler stage\\n \\n-The Aug-10 coverage sweep mapped which battery stage semantically checks\\n-each test tree: `blade/tests` and `libs/*/tests` run inside `bootstrap`\\n-(and ONLY there — `--quick` skips them; `sos/tests` gated inside the\\n-`sos` stage until both left for the sawos repo at 238 unit 5), and\\n-`selfhost/lexer/tests` ran NOWHERE — nine passing tests no stage\\n-compiled. `selfhostlex` closes that: each `selfhost/lexer/tests/*.saw`\\n-is compiled and run, exit 0 = pass, same contract as `blade test`.\\n-(`tests/freestanding` joined that map with the `freestanding` stage above,\\n-and runs there only.)\\n+Runs `compiler/tests/run.py`, the self-hosted compiler's tests (above). It is\\n+where the compiler's unit programs are compiled and run; no other stage opens\\n+them. For the other test trees: `blade/tests` and `libs/*/tests` run inside\\n+`bootstrap` only (so `--quick` skips them), and `tests/freestanding` runs inside\\n+the `freestanding` stage only.\\n \\n ### The forgetgate stage\\n \\n@@ -1312,7 +1365,7 @@ Put the token where the client can find it (`~/.config/saw-worker/token`, or\\n # itself is Saw (devtools/irdet/); this driver builds it and splits the corpus.\\n ./.venv/bin/python tools/irdet_remote.py --all --remote studio.local:8710\\n \\n-# The whole battery on the worker: suite, lexdiff, astdiff, irdet --all\\n+# The whole battery on the worker: suite, compiler, astdiff, irdet --all\\n ./.venv/bin/python tools/remote_battery.py --remote studio.local:8710\\n ```\\n \\ndiff --git a/build.sh b/build.sh\\nindex 5d2607ee..fe3d0b5b 100755\\n--- a/build.sh\\n+++ b/build.sh\\n@@ -1,23 +1,44 @@\\n #!/bin/sh\\n # The sawtracker patch gate's entry point: `.sawtracker/tests.json` runs\\n-# `./build.sh test` on every submitted patch, in the server's own clone.\\n-# Also fine to run by hand. Bootstraps .venv when absent, then runs the\\n-# compiler's per-commit gate — full suite + the freestanding feature\\n-# suite, both arches — under the machine-wide suite lock (TESTING.md)\\n-# so a server-side patch test never overlaps a local suite run.\\n+# `./build.sh test --changed-since HEAD^` on every submitted patch, in the\\n+# server's own clone, where HEAD is the applied patch and HEAD^ its base. Also\\n+# fine to run by hand:\\n+#\\n+#   ./build.sh test                        everything\\n+#   ./build.sh test --changed-since REV    what the change since REV needs\\n+#   ./build.sh test --diff FILE            what a patch file needs\\n+#   ./build.sh test ... --dry-run          print that decision, run nothing\\n+#\\n+# tools/patch_gate.py decides which suites a change needs and runs them. This\\n+# script bootstraps .venv when absent (SAW_PYTHON names an interpreter to use\\n+# instead, as from a worktree) and holds the machine-wide suite lock around the\\n+# run (TESTING.md), so a server-side patch test never overlaps a local suite.\\n set -e\\n \\n cd \\\"$(dirname \\\"$0\\\")\\\"\\n \\n case \\\"${1:-test}\\\" in\\n-  test) ;;\\n-  *) echo \\\"usage: ./build.sh test\\\" >&2; exit 2 ;;\\n+  test) if [ $# -gt 0 ]; then shift; fi ;;\\n+  *) echo \\\"usage: ./build.sh test [--changed-since REV | --diff FILE] [--dry-run]\\\" >&2\\n+     exit 2 ;;\\n esac\\n \\n-if [ ! -x .venv/bin/python ]; then\\n-    python3 -m venv .venv\\n-    .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet --upgrade pip\\n-    .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet -r sawc/requirements.txt\\n+# A dry run reads git and nothing else, so it needs neither the venv nor the lock.\\n+for arg in \\\"$@\\\"; do\\n+    if [ \\\"$arg\\\" = \\\"--dry-run\\\" ]; then\\n+        exec \\\"${SAW_PYTHON:-python3}\\\" tools/patch_gate.py \\\"$@\\\"\\n+    fi\\n+done\\n+\\n+if [ -n \\\"${SAW_PYTHON:-}\\\" ]; then\\n+    PY=\\\"$SAW_PYTHON\\\"\\n+else\\n+    if [ ! -x .venv/bin/python ]; then\\n+        python3 -m venv .venv\\n+        .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet --upgrade pip\\n+        .venv/bin/python -m pip install --quiet -r sawc/requirements.txt\\n+    fi\\n+    PY=.venv/bin/python\\n fi\\n \\n LOCK=\\\"/private/tmp/claude-$(id -u)/saw-suite-lock\\\"\\n@@ -25,5 +46,4 @@ mkdir -p \\\"$(dirname \\\"$LOCK\\\")\\\"\\n until mkdir \\\"$LOCK\\\" 2>/dev/null; do sleep 15; done\\n trap 'rmdir \\\"$LOCK\\\"' EXIT INT TERM\\n \\n-.venv/bin/python test_runner.py\\n-.venv/bin/python tools/freestanding_runner.py\\n+\\\"$PY\\\" tools/patch_gate.py \\\"$@\\\"\\ndiff --git a/compiler/README.md b/compiler/README.md\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..d5ece589\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/README.md\\n@@ -0,0 +1,172 @@\\n+# compiler/: the self-hosted Saw compiler\\n+\\n+The Saw compiler written in Saw (epic SL-398). Until it builds itself, the\\n+frozen Python compiler in `sawc/` builds it: that is bootstrap Stage 0. The\\n+design lives in the tracker's docs, which `~/bin/sawtracker doc show NAME`\\n+prints:\\n+\\n+- **SL:architecture**: the stages and their contracts (§3), the shared\\n+  representation (§3.0), and the bootstrap and the subset (§4).\\n+- **SL:hazards**: the shapes Stage 0 mishandles, and which of them the subset\\n+  checker refuses.\\n+- **SL:testing** and **SL:borrowing**: the locked-down language the compiler\\n+  implements.\\n+\\n+## Layout\\n+\\n+```\\n+compiler/\\n+  lex/                the lexer, package `sawlex` (lex/README.md has its dump format)\\n+    src/lib.saw\\n+    tests/*.saw       unit programs\\n+  driver/             the `sawc2` binary, package `sawc2`\\n+    src/main.saw\\n+  tools/\\n+    build.py          builds sawc2, or any program over the stage packages\\n+    subset_check.py   the subset checker\\n+  tests/\\n+    run.py            the test runner\\n+    lex/              golden token fixtures\\n+    subset/           the subset checker's own fixtures\\n+```\\n+\\n+Each stage has its own directory and is a package, with its source under\\n+`src/`. The others import it as `<package>.src.<module>`, through\\n+`--module-path`. `STAGE_PACKAGES` in `tools/build.py` is the one table of those\\n+packages, and every build goes through `build_program` there. The driver is\\n+named `sawc2` for now.\\n+\\n+## Building\\n+\\n+Use the venv's Python, since it runs the frozen compiler:\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py            # builds .build/sawc2\\n+.build/sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>                   # the token dump, or the doc trivia\\n+.build/sawc2 lex --kinds                               # every token kind's dump name\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py ENTRY.saw -o OUT   # another program\\n+```\\n+\\n+A lex error prints one `ERROR` record and exits 1. A usage or I/O failure exits 2.\\n+\\n+## Testing\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tests/run.py\\n+```\\n+\\n+The runner prints each failure on its own line, then one summary line, and exits\\n+1 if anything failed. Its output is deterministic. The battery's `compiler` lane\\n+runs it, and so does every per-patch gate run (`./build.sh test`). It checks four\\n+things.\\n+\\n+- **Unit programs**: `compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`. These are small programs in\\n+  the subset, built by the frozen compiler against the stage packages. Each\\n+  passes by exiting 0. Until the compiler builds itself, these and the golden\\n+  dumps are its only tests. At self-hosting, `*.test.saw` sidecars take over\\n+  (SL:testing §4).\\n+- **Golden token fixtures**: `tests/lex/NAME.saw`, with the expected\\n+  `sawc2 lex` output in `NAME.tokens`. Where doc trivia matter, `NAME.docs` holds\\n+  the expected `sawc2 lex --docs` output. Each fixture's first line names its\\n+  source, which is a tracked `.saw` file or the inputs of a lexer unit program.\\n+  The fixtures are the lexer's oracle. When the lexer changes on purpose,\\n+  regenerate the affected expectations with `sawc2 lex` and review the diff.\\n+  No fixture line may end in whitespace, and no fixture may end in a blank line:\\n+  the patch server's `git apply --whitespace=fix` and most editors strip both,\\n+  so the runner refuses them by file and line.\\n+- **Coverage**: every token kind appears in some `.tokens` file. The kinds come\\n+  from the lexer itself: `sawc2 lex --kinds` prints `token_kinds()`, and the\\n+  runner checks its length against the `TokenKind` cases as declared. Every kind\\n+  of lex error appears too, taken from the messages of the lexer's own\\n+  `err`/`err_at` calls.\\n+- **The subset checker**, over the compiler's source and over its own fixtures\\n+  (below).\\n+\\n+## The subset\\n+\\n+The compiler's own source is written in the subset of SL:architecture §4. It is\\n+the intersection of today's Saw and the locked-down language, with the shapes\\n+Stage 0 mishandles taken out. `tools/subset_check.py` enforces it over every\\n+`compiler/**/*.saw` except `compiler/tests/**` (fixtures hold arbitrary Saw) and\\n+`*.test.saw` (sidecars, which only Stage 1 builds). It reads the source through\\n+the frozen compiler's own lexer and parser, so it checks exactly what Stage 0\\n+reads.\\n+\\n+```sh\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/subset_check.py              # the tree\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/subset_check.py FILE...      # these files\\n+```\\n+\\n+A diagnostic reads `file:line: rule: message (source)`. The driver and the stage\\n+packages form one build, and each unit program is a build of its own over the\\n+stage packages. That matters for the rules that compare declarations across a\\n+build. Each rule has a fixture, `tests/subset/RULE.saw`, whose\\n+`// refuses: RULE...` markers name exactly the lines and rules the checker must\\n+report. `tests/subset/clean.saw` must report nothing. The runner fails for a\\n+rule with no fixture.\\n+\\n+| Rule | Refuses | Source |\\n+|---|---|---|\\n+| `lex`, `parse` | a file the frozen lexer or parser rejects | §4 |\\n+| `file-end` | a file that does not end in a newline | C2 |\\n+| `sync-only` | tasks, threads, channels, `sleep`, a `blocking` extern | §4 |\\n+| `closure-capture` | a closure naming an enclosing binding or `self`, a capture list | §4 |\\n+| `type-alias` | any `type` alias | §4, S9 |\\n+| `prelude-type-name` | a declared type named like a prelude type | §4, L17 |\\n+| `selective-imports` | `import m` and `import m.*` | §4, L3 |\\n+| `import-allowlist` | a std module off `ALLOWED_STD_MODULES`, or a package that is not a compiler stage | §4 |\\n+| `std-api` | a std call off `STD_API`; `m[k]` on a Map; a write or presence test through `get`; `m[k]! = v`; `with_ref`, `with_var_ref`, `with_unique`, a closure-taking `lock` | §4 |\\n+| `generic-extension-init` | an `init` in an extension with type parameters | §4, S1 |\\n+| `integer-overload` | an overload set where a member takes a non-`Int` integer | §4, L1 |\\n+| `any-type` | `any` in a type | §4, S12 |\\n+| `box-type` | `Box` | §4, L8 |\\n+| `cell-type` | `Mutex`, `SpinLock`, `Once`, `UnsafeMutableInterior`, `Atomic`, `Arc` | §4, S11, S15 |\\n+| `raw-pointer` | pointer types, std's `unsafe struct`s, `unsafe`, `*p`, `move *p` | §4, C1 |\\n+| `fixed-array` | `[T; N]` types and repeat literals | §4, L7 |\\n+| `value-loop` | `break` with an operand | §4, L11 |\\n+| `statement-arm` | a match arm whose body is a bare statement | §4, L13, C4 |\\n+| `borrow-syntax` | `borrow` as a name | §4 |\\n+| `test-directive` | `@test` | §4 |\\n+| `borrowed-match-payload` | `move` of an arm binding, or a `match` on one, when the scrutinee is borrowed | S2 |\\n+| `try-ownership` | an unbound `try ... catch`, `move` and `try` in one statement, `move` in a `catch` | S3 |\\n+| `root-reuse` | an argument reading the receiver's or a `&` argument's root; an index write whose right side reads its root | S4 |\\n+| `var-ref-into-let` | `&var` into a binding that is not a `var` | S5 |\\n+| `function-exit` | a value-returning body that can fall off its end; code after `return`, `break` or `continue` | S6 |\\n+| `int-literal-range` | an unsuffixed integer literal above `Int.max` | S7 |\\n+| `enum-equatable-body` | a hand-written `Equatable` on an enum | S8 |\\n+| `nested-optional` | `T??`, and an optional element or value type in `Vector`, `Map` or `Set` | S10 |\\n+| `explicit-nested-type-args` | an explicit, itself generic, type argument at a construction | S13 |\\n+| `field-nested-generic` | a field or payload type nesting a generic whose defaulted parameters are unwritten | §4, S13 |\\n+| `argument-labels` | a repeated label; a labeled call to a std `borrows` accessor | S14 |\\n+| `index-receiver-call` | a method on `x[i]` that is not known to only read | S15 |\\n+| `cast-then-optional` | `?` or `??` right after an `as` cast's type | S17 |\\n+| `program-unique-names` | a free function or `static` named like another in the build or in std | S18 |\\n+| `written-type-shape` | a written type nested 8 levels, `Optional<T>`, `Self` inside a tuple type | §4, S19 |\\n+| `float-literal` | any float literal | §4, S20 |\\n+| `from-raw-literal` | `from(raw:)` with a bare literal | L1 |\\n+| `closure-syntax` | an unannotated closure parameter, `$0`, a trailing closure, a function type under `?` | L2 |\\n+| `default-value-literal` | a default parameter value that is not a literal | L3 |\\n+| `optional-shaping` | `== None`, a method directly on a `get` result, a collection literal returned as a `Result` | L4 |\\n+| `empty-struct` | a struct with no fields | L5 |\\n+| `bounded-extension` | a bound on an extension's type parameter | L6 |\\n+| `name-collision` | a generic method and generic function sharing a name (std included), a static and an instance method sharing one, a type parameter spelled like a type | L9 |\\n+| `type-param-receiver` | a call whose receiver is a type parameter | L10 |\\n+| `leading-minus` | a line that begins with `-` | L12 |\\n+| `nesting-depth`, `chain-length` | brackets nested past 30; an operator, postfix or `else if` chain past 100 | L14 |\\n+| `interpolation-content` | `//`, a brace or a quote inside an interpolation | C3 |\\n+\\n+§4 is SL:architecture §4; the other codes are SL:hazards entries.\\n+\\n+Several checks are syntactic, so they are partial. What they cannot see stays a\\n+trust obligation, as SL:hazards describes:\\n+\\n+- `std-api` cannot know a receiver's type. So an instance method on the\\n+  allowlist is allowed on every receiver, and a call counts as std's only when\\n+  the build declares no function, method or type of that name. `m[k]` is caught\\n+  when the receiver's written type is traceable to `Map`, or when the subscript\\n+  is used as an optional.\\n+- `closure-capture`, `root-reuse` and `var-ref-into-let` resolve names through\\n+  lexical scopes. They do not follow values.\\n+- `nested-optional` and `field-nested-generic` see written types only, not the\\n+  types a generic instantiation produces.\\n+- SL:hazards S16 and L15 have no check.\\ndiff --git a/compiler/driver/Saw.toml b/compiler/driver/Saw.toml\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..be7e9fc8\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/driver/Saw.toml\\n@@ -0,0 +1,6 @@\\n+[package]\\n+name = \\\"sawc2\\\"\\n+version = \\\"0.1.0\\\"\\n+\\n+[dependencies]\\n+sawlex = { path = \\\"../lex\\\" }\\ndiff --git a/compiler/driver/src/main.saw b/compiler/driver/src/main.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..f89c04e2\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/driver/src/main.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,146 @@\\n+// sawc2, the driver of the self-hosted Saw compiler. Each stage is reached\\n+// through a subcommand; the lexer is the first.\\n+//\\n+//   sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>   the token dump, or the doc-comment trivia\\n+//   sawc2 lex --kinds               every token kind's dump name, one per line\\n+//\\n+// The record formats are the lexer's canonical dump (compiler/lex/README.md).\\n+// A lex error prints one ERROR record and exits 1; a usage or I/O failure\\n+// prints a message and exits 2.\\n+\\n+import sawlex.src.lib.{LexResult, format_doc, format_error, format_token, kind_name, lex, token_kinds}\\n+import std.env.{Env}\\n+import std.file.{File}\\n+import std.path.{Path}\\n+\\n+static DRIVER_EXIT_LEX_ERROR: Int = 1\\n+static DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE: Int = 2\\n+\\n+func driver_usage() -> Int {\\n+    print(\\\"usage: sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"       sawc2 lex --kinds\\\")\\n+    DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() -> Int {\\n+    let args = try! Env.args()\\n+    let command = args.get(1) ?? \\\"\\\"\\n+    if command.equals(\\\"lex\\\") {\\n+        return driver_lex(&args)\\n+    }\\n+    driver_usage()\\n+}\\n+\\n+// `sawc2 lex`: the arguments after the subcommand name one file, or ask for\\n+// the kind list.\\n+func driver_lex(args: &Vector<String>) -> Int {\\n+    var docs_mode = false\\n+    var kinds_mode = false\\n+    var path = \\\"\\\"\\n+    var i = 2\\n+    while i < args.len() {\\n+        let arg = args.get(i) ?? \\\"\\\"\\n+        if arg.equals(\\\"--docs\\\") {\\n+            docs_mode = true\\n+        } else if arg.equals(\\\"--kinds\\\") {\\n+            kinds_mode = true\\n+        } else if arg.starts_with(\\\"--\\\") || not path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+            return driver_usage()\\n+        } else {\\n+            path = arg\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+    if kinds_mode {\\n+        if docs_mode || not path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+            return driver_usage()\\n+        }\\n+        return driver_print_kinds()\\n+    }\\n+    if path.equals(\\\"\\\") {\\n+        return driver_usage()\\n+    }\\n+    if let text = driver_read_source(path) {\\n+        driver_dump(text, docs_mode)\\n+    } else {\\n+        DRIVER_EXIT_USAGE\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The file's text, or None once the reason it could not be read is printed.\\n+func driver_read_source(path: String) -> String? {\\n+    let opened = File.open(Path(s: path))\\n+    var file = match opened {\\n+        case Ok(handle) -> move handle,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: cannot open {path}: {e}\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    let read = file.read()\\n+    let data = match read {\\n+        case Ok(bytes) -> move bytes,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: cannot read {path}: {e}\\\")\\n+            return None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+    let decoded = data.to_string()\\n+    match decoded {\\n+        case Ok(text) -> text,\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(\\\"sawc2: {path} is not valid UTF-8 (byte {e.offset})\\\")\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_dump(source: String, docs_mode: Bool) -> Int {\\n+    let lexed = lex(source)\\n+    match lexed {\\n+        case Ok(result) -> {\\n+            if docs_mode {\\n+                driver_print_docs(&result)\\n+            } else {\\n+                driver_print_tokens(&result)\\n+            }\\n+            0\\n+        },\\n+        case Err(e) -> {\\n+            print(format_error(&e))\\n+            DRIVER_EXIT_LEX_ERROR\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_tokens(result: &LexResult) {\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < result.tokens.len() {\\n+        if let t = result.tokens.get(i) {\\n+            print(format_token(&t, &result.segments))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_docs(result: &LexResult) {\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < result.docs.len() {\\n+        if let d = result.docs.get(i) {\\n+            print(format_doc(&d))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func driver_print_kinds() -> Int {\\n+    let kinds = token_kinds()\\n+    var i = 0\\n+    while i < kinds.len() {\\n+        if let k = kinds.get(i) {\\n+            print(kind_name(k))\\n+        }\\n+        i = i + 1\\n+    }\\n+    0\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/compiler/lex/README.md b/compiler/lex/README.md\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..daa8100a\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/lex/README.md\\n@@ -0,0 +1,174 @@\\n+# sawlex: the Saw lexer, in Saw\\n+\\n+The lexer stage of the self-hosted compiler (`compiler/README.md`). It produces\\n+the same token kinds, token boundaries, 1-based `line:col` positions and lex-error\\n+positions as the Python lexer in `sawc/lexer.py`. `LANGUAGE_SPEC.md`'s lexical\\n+section is authoritative where the two disagree. The golden fixtures in\\n+`compiler/tests/lex/` pin its output.\\n+\\n+## Layout\\n+\\n+```\\n+compiler/lex/\\n+  Saw.toml          # package manifest (name = \\\"sawlex\\\")\\n+  src/lib.saw       # the token model (TokenKind, Token, StringSegment), `lex`, the dump format\\n+  tests/*.saw       # unit programs, one per token family\\n+```\\n+\\n+The CLI is the driver's `lex` subcommand:\\n+\\n+```\\n+./.venv/bin/python compiler/tools/build.py\\n+.build/sawc2 lex [--docs] <file.saw>\\n+.build/sawc2 lex --kinds\\n+```\\n+\\n+## Canonical token-dump format\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex <file.saw>` emits one record per token, newline-separated:\\n+\\n+```\\n+KIND<TAB>line:col[<TAB>escaped-text][<TAB>suffix]\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `KIND` is the token kind's dump name (see the mapping table below).\\n+* `line:col` is 1-based, at the token's first byte or character.\\n+* `escaped-text` is the token's canonical text (decoded for strings; underscores\\n+  stripped and the base prefix lowercased for numbers), escaped at the **byte**\\n+  level: `\\\\\\\\` for backslash, `\\\\n` `\\\\t` `\\\\r` for those controls, `\\\\0` for NUL, any\\n+  other control byte (`< 0x20` or `0x7F`) as `\\\\xHH`, a space that ends the text\\n+  as `\\\\x20`, and every other byte, including raw UTF-8 `>= 0x80`, verbatim.\\n+* `suffix` is a 4th column, present **only** for a fixed-width-suffixed integer\\n+  literal: one of `i8`/`i16`/`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`u16`/`u32`/`u64`. `255u8` dumps as\\n+  `INT<TAB>1:1<TAB>255<TAB>u8`; every other token stops at the escaped text.\\n+\\n+**No record ends in whitespace.** When the last field of a record is empty, it is\\n+left out together with the tab before it: the end-of-file token dumps as\\n+`EOF<TAB>6:1`, an empty string as `STRING<TAB>1:1`, and an empty doc line as\\n+`DOC<TAB>8:1<TAB>doc`. An empty field with another field after it keeps its tab,\\n+since that tab does not end the record. And a space that ends an escaped field\\n+is written `\\\\x20`, so `\\\"a \\\"` dumps as `STRING<TAB>1:1<TAB>a\\\\x20`. Tools that strip\\n+trailing whitespace, such as `git apply --whitespace=fix` and most editors,\\n+therefore leave every record intact.\\n+\\n+The format changed to this after the one-time comparison with the Python\\n+lexer's dump (SL-399). That comparison was made in the earlier format, where\\n+such a record ended in a tab or a space. The golden fixtures were regenerated in\\n+this format, and they are the oracle.\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex --kinds` prints every kind's dump name, one per line, in the order\\n+`TokenKind` declares them.\\n+\\n+### An `INTERP_STRING` record carries its segments\\n+\\n+An interpolated string's `escaped-text` is the literal's **raw source spelling**\\n+between the quotes (undecoded), and the record then continues with **one field\\n+per typed segment** (design 268), in source order:\\n+\\n+```\\n+INTERP_STRING<TAB>line:col<TAB>raw<TAB>T:text<TAB>E:line:col:raw-expr...\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `T:` is a literal run, **decoded** (so an escaped brace is a plain `{`/`}`).\\n+* `E:line:col:` is an interpolation, carrying the **raw** text between its braces\\n+  and the exact 1-based source position of its opening `{`.\\n+\\n+Both are escaped as the text field is, final space included.\\n+\\n+An empty `T:` run is never emitted, so two adjacent interpolations produce two\\n+consecutive `E:` fields. No token carries both a suffix and segments, so the\\n+tail is unambiguous. The segments are what a parser consumes: nothing is encoded\\n+into the token's bytes.\\n+\\n+On a lex error the CLI emits a single record and exits 1:\\n+\\n+```\\n+ERROR<TAB>line:col<TAB>message\\n+```\\n+\\n+The position and the kind of error match the Python lexer. The message is this\\n+lexer's own wording.\\n+\\n+## Doc-comment trivia dump\\n+\\n+`sawc2 lex --docs <file.saw>` emits the documentation comments instead of the\\n+token stream, one record per captured line:\\n+\\n+```\\n+DOC<TAB>line:col<TAB>kind[<TAB>escaped-text]\\n+```\\n+\\n+* `kind` is `doc` for a `///` line and `module` for a `//!` line.\\n+* `line:col` is 1-based, at the leading `/`.\\n+* `escaped-text` is the line body with the `///`/`//!` marker and one following\\n+  space stripped, escaped by the same byte-level scheme as a token's text. An\\n+  empty body is left out with its tab.\\n+\\n+Doc comments are **trivia**: `lex` skips them exactly as it skips `//` comments,\\n+so the token dump above is unaffected. `lex` returns one `LexResult` carrying\\n+the tokens, the doc records and the string-segment arena together. There is no\\n+tokens-only entry point, because a token's `seg_start`/`seg_count` index into\\n+that arena. Only a comment that starts its line is a doc comment; `////`\\n+(four or more slashes) and a `///` trailing code on the same line are ordinary\\n+comments.\\n+\\n+## Kind-name mapping (TokenKind case to dump name)\\n+\\n+| TokenKind case | dump name | TokenKind case | dump name |\\n+|---|---|---|---|\\n+| `IntLit` | `INT` | `WrapAdd` | `WRAP_ADD` |\\n+| `FloatLit` | `FLOAT` | `WrapSub` | `WRAP_SUB` |\\n+| `StringLit` | `STRING` | `WrapMul` | `WRAP_MUL` |\\n+| `InterpString` | `INTERP_STRING` | `Not_` | `NOT` |\\n+| `Ident` | `IDENT` | `Move_` | `MOVE` |\\n+| `Func` | `FUNC` | `Unsafe_` | `UNSAFE` |\\n+| `Let` | `LET` | `Borrows` | `BORROWS` |\\n+| `Var` | `VAR` | `Lend` | `LEND` |\\n+| `If` | `IF` | `Assign` | `ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Else` | `ELSE` | `PlusAssign` | `PLUS_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Guard` | `GUARD` | `MinusAssign` | `MINUS_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Return` | `RETURN` | `StarAssign` | `STAR_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `True_` | `TRUE` | `SlashAssign` | `SLASH_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `False_` | `FALSE` | `PercentAssign` | `PERCENT_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Struct` | `STRUCT` | `AmpAssign` | `AMP_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Extension` | `EXTENSION` | `PipeAssign` | `PIPE_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `SelfKw` | `SELF` | `CaretAssign` | `CARET_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Init` | `INIT` | `ShlAssign` | `SHL_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `NoneKw` | `NONE` | `ShrAssign` | `SHR_ASSIGN` |\\n+| `Enum` | `ENUM` | `Question` | `QUESTION` |\\n+| `Case` | `CASE` | `DoubleQuestion` | `DOUBLE_QUESTION` |\\n+| `Match` | `MATCH` | `Exclaim` | `EXCLAIM` |\\n+| `While` | `WHILE` | `QuestionDot` | `QUESTION_DOT` |\\n+| `Break` | `BREAK` | `DotDot` | `DOTDOT` |\\n+| `Continue` | `CONTINUE` | `DotDotEq` | `DOTDOT_EQ` |\\n+| `Trait` | `TRAIT` | `Ellipsis` | `ELLIPSIS` |\\n+| `For` | `FOR` | `LParen` | `LPAREN` |\\n+| `In` | `IN` | `RParen` | `RPAREN` |\\n+| `Extern` | `EXTERN` | `LBrace` | `LBRACE` |\\n+| `As` | `AS` | `RBrace` | `RBRACE` |\\n+| `Try` | `TRY` | `LBracket` | `LBRACKET` |\\n+| `Catch` | `CATCH` | `RBracket` | `RBRACKET` |\\n+| `Static` | `STATIC` | `Comma` | `COMMA` |\\n+| `Public` | `PUBLIC` | `Colon` | `COLON` |\\n+| `Plus` … `Tilde` | `PLUS` … `TILDE` | `Semicolon` | `SEMICOLON` |\\n+| `HashDirective` | `HASH_DIRECTIVE` | `Arrow` | `ARROW` |\\n+| `DollarParam` | `DOLLAR_PARAM` | `Dot` | `DOT` |\\n+| `Newline` | `NEWLINE` | `At` | `AT` |\\n+| `Eof` | `EOF` | | |\\n+\\n+The remaining single-character operators map by uppercasing:\\n+`Minus`→`MINUS`, `Star`→`STAR`, `Slash`→`SLASH`, `Percent`→`PERCENT`,\\n+`Eq`→`EQ`, `Neq`→`NEQ`, `Lt`→`LT`, `Gt`→`GT`, `Lte`→`LTE`, `Gte`→`GTE`,\\n+`And`→`AND`, `Or`→`OR`, `Ampersand`→`AMPERSAND`, `Pipe`→`PIPE`,\\n+`Caret`→`CARET`, `Tilde`→`TILDE`. The words `module`, `import`, `export`,\\n+`package`, `parent` and `type` are not keywords: each lexes as `IDENT`, and the\\n+parser decides by position.\\n+\\n+## How it reads the source\\n+\\n+The lexer works over the source's UTF-8 **bytes** while counting columns by code\\n+point (a column advances once per non-continuation byte). That gives the\\n+per-code-point columns positions are defined in, and lets token text be\\n+assembled from raw bytes. Taken from examples/borrowing_producer_every_path.saw.\\n+// K144 accept twin (design 275 U3, codex r1 #1): the origin check follows\\n+// EVERY returning path, so a producer whose every leaf — a `return`, each\\n+// `match` arm, and each arm of an `if` nested in one — constructs the struct\\n+// with `lends self` is accepted, and each spelling produces a working window.\\n+//\\n+// K143 CONTROLS (codex r4 #1): the borrowing walk descends a function type's\\n+// return and parameters, and a callable that names NO borrowing struct is\\n+// untouched by it — a reference in a closure PARAMETER stays legal (that is\\n+// the no-escape walk's exemption, which the borrowing walk does not share),\\n+// and a closure literal inside a window body is an ordinary closure.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// return path 6\\n+// match arm 6\\n+// nested if 5\\n+// total 17\\n+// callable controls 3 12\\n+\\n+struct Bag {\\n+    private items: Vector<Int>\\n+}\\n+extension Bag: NoCopy {}\\n+\\n+borrows struct BagIterator {\\n+    private bag: &Bag\\n+    private index: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func pick(&self, k: Int, from_one: Bool) borrows -> BagIterator {\\n+        if k == 0 { return BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0) }\\n+        match k {\\n+            case 1 -> BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0),\\n+            case _ -> {\\n+                if from_one { BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 1) }\\n+                else { BagIterator(bag: lends self, index: 0) }\\n+            }\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension BagIterator: Iterator {\\n+    type Item = Int\\n+    public func next(&var self) -> Int? {\\n+        if self.index < self.bag.items.len() {\\n+            let i = self.index\\n+            self.index = self.index + 1\\n+            self.bag.items.get(i)\\n+        } else {\\n+            None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func sum(b: &Bag, k: Int, from_one: Bool) -> Int {\\n+    var total = 0\\n+    for x in b.pick(k, from_one) { total = total + x }\\n+    total\\n+}\\n+\\n+// A reference in a closure parameter: legal, and the borrowing walk that now\\n+// descends `(&Bag) sync -> Int` finds no borrowing struct in it.\\n+func through(b: &Bag, body: (&Bag) sync -> Int) -> Int { body(&b) }\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var v: Vector<Int> = [1, 2, 3]\\n+    let bag = Bag(items: move v)\\n+    let a = sum(&bag, 0, false)\\n+    let b = sum(&bag, 1, false)\\n+    let c = sum(&bag, 2, true)\\n+    print(\\\"return path {a}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"match arm {b}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"nested if {c}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"total {a + b + c}\\\")\\n+    let d = through(&bag, { g in g.items.len() })\\n+    let twice = { n: Int in n * 2 }\\n+    var e = 0\\n+    for x in bag.pick(0, false) { e = e + twice(x) }\\n+    print(\\\"callable controls {d} {e}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/compiler/tests/lex/borrowing_producer_every_path.tokens --git a/compiler/tests/lex/vector_in_erased_result.saw b/compiler/tests/lex/vector_in_erased_result.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..68324a05\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/tests/lex/vector_in_erased_result.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,61 @@\\n+// Fixture: a tracked file, copied whole. Taken from examples/vector_in_erased_result.saw.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// 2\\n+// a\\n+// b\\n+// err7\\n+\\n+// Regression (design 67 / dogfood DF6): a generic type with a DEFAULT type param\\n+// used with fewer args (`Vector<Dependency>` = `Vector<Dependency, GlobalAllocator>`) must\\n+// fill that default consistently BEFORE mangling, including when NESTED inside\\n+// another generic (a `Result` Ok type). Previously the default-arg filling\\n+// diverged — `Vector$1$Dependency` in one path, `Vector$2$Dependency$GlobalAllocator` in\\n+// another — so `Result<Vector<Dependency>, Box<any Error>>` registered the enum\\n+// under one mangled name and looked it up under the other, ICE'ing the err-return\\n+// path (`KeyError: Result$...Vector$1$Dependency...`). Now the arities agree.\\n+\\n+struct Dependency {\\n+    name: String\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct MyError {\\n+    code: Int\\n+}\\n+\\n+extension MyError: Error {\\n+    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) {\\n+        try! into.append(\\\"err\\\")\\n+        try! into.append(self.code)\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// Ok type `Vector<Dependency>` (arity-1 written), erased-error Result.\\n+func build(ok: Bool) -> Result<Vector<Dependency>, Box<any Error>> {\\n+    if not ok {\\n+        return MyError(code: 7)\\n+    }\\n+    var v = Vector<Dependency>()\\n+    try! v.push(Dependency(name: \\\"a\\\"))\\n+    try! v.push(Dependency(name: \\\"b\\\"))\\n+    return move v\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    match build(true) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> {\\n+            print(v.len())\\n+            var i = 0\\n+            while i < v.len() {\\n+                guard let d = v.get(i) else { break }\\n+                print(d.name)\\n+                i = i + 1\\n+            }\\n+        },\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"{e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+    match build(false) {\\n+        case Ok(v) -> print(v.len()),\\n+        case Err(e) -> print(\\\"{e}\\\")\\n+    }\\n+}\\ndiff --git self.failures.append(text)\\n+\\n+    def count(self, key, n=1):\\n+        self.counts[key] = self.counts.get(key, 0) + n\\n+\\n+\\n+def unit_programs():\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`compiler/<stage>/tests/*.saw`, as (name, path), sorted.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    out = []\\n+    for path in glob.glob(os.path.join(COMPILER, \\\"*\\\", \\\"tests\\\", \\\"*.saw\\\")):\\n+ None).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    exe = os.path.join(UNIT_OUT, name.replace(\\\"/\\\", \\\"_\\\"))\\n+    ok, output = build.build_program(path, exe)\\n+    if not ok:\\n+        return name, \\\"unit %s: name, \\\"unit %s: exit %d: %s\\\" % (name, r.returncode, detail)\\n+    return name, None\\n+\\n+\\n+def _first_line(text):\\n+    for line in (text or \\\"\\\").splitlines():\\n+        if (\\\".docs\\\", (\\\"--docs\\\",))):\\n+            exp_path = stem + suffix\\n+            if not os.path.exists(exp_path):\\n+                continue\\n+            with open(exp_path, \\\"rb\\\") if got != expected:\\n+                run.fail(\\\"golden %s%s: output differs at %s\\\"\\n+                         % (rel, \\\" --docs\\\" if flags else \\\"\\\", _first_difference(expected, got)))\\n+            elif code != want_code:\\n+                run.fail(\\\"golden %s%s: exit %d, expected %d\\\"\\n+                         % (rel, \\\" --docs\\\" if flags else \\\"\\\", code, want_code))\\n+            run.count(\\\"golden expectations\\\")\\n+            if suffix == \\\".tokens\\\":\\n+                for record in expected.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\"):\\n+             elif fields[0]:\\n+                        kinds_seen.add(fields[0].decode(\\\"ascii\\\"))\\n+        run.count(\\\"golden fixtures\\\")\\n+    check_kind_coverage(run, kinds_seen)\\n+    check_error_coverage(run, errors_seen)\\n+\\n+\\n+def _first_difference(expected, got):\\n+    exp_lines = expected.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n+    got_lines = got.split(b\\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n+    for i in range(max(len(exp_lines), len(got_lines))):\\n+        a = exp_lines[i] if i < len(exp_lines) else b\\\"<end>\\\"\\n+        b = got_lines[i] if i < len(got_lines) else b\\\"<end>\\\"\\n+        if a != b:\\n+            return \\\"record %d: expected %r, got %r\\\" % (i + 1, a[:80], b[:80])\\n+    return \\\"a trailing byte\\\"\\n+\\n+\\n+def lexer_program():\\n+    src = subset_check.SourceFile(LEXER_SOURCE)\\n+    if src.program is None:\\n+        raise RuntimeError(\\\"cannot parse %s\\\" % os.path.relpath(LEXER_SOURCE, REPO))\\n+    return src.program\\n+\\n+\\n+def check_kind_coverage(run, kinds_seen):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The kinds come from the lexer: `sawc2 lex --kinds`, whose length must\\n+    match the case count of `TokenKind` as declared.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    code, out = sawc2(\\\"lex\\\", \\\"--kinds\\\")\\n+    kinds = out.decode(\\\"ascii\\\").split()\\n+    if code != 0 or not kinds:\\n+        run.fail(\\\"coverage: `sawc2 a lex error at %s:%d has no literal message\\\"\\n+                         % (os.path.relpath(LEXER_SOURCE, REPO), n.line))\\n+                continue\\n+            messages.add(first.value)\\n+    for message in sorted(messages):\\n+        if message not in errors_seen:\\n+            run.fail(\\\"coverage: lex error %r appears in no golden fixture\\\" % message)\\n+    run.count(\\\"lex error kinds\\\", len(messages))\\n+\\n+\\n+def run_subset(run):\\n+    files, diags = subset_check.check_tree()\\n+    for d in diags:\\n+        run.fail(\\\"subset: \\\" + d.render())\\n+    run.count(\\\"checked source files\\\", len(files))\\n+    fixtures = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(SUBSET_FIXTURES, \\\"*.saw\\\")))\\n+    names = {os.path.splitext(os.path.basename(p))[0] for p in fixtures}\\n+    for rule in sorted(subset_check.RULES):\\n+        if rule not in names:\\n+            run.fail(\\\"subset: rule %s has no fixture\\\" % rule)\\n+    for path in fixtures:\\n+ compiler (Stage 0).\\n+\\n+    python compiler/tools/build.py                   # .build/sawc2\\n+    python compiler/tools/build.py ENTRY.saw -o OUT  # any program over the stages\\n+\\n+The interpreter running this script runs `sawc/sawc.py`, so invoke it with the\\n+venv's Python. `STAGE_PACKAGES` is the one place that maps a stage package to\\n+its directory: the driver, the unit programs and the runner all build through\\n+`build_program`, so a new stage is registered here once.\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+import argparse\\n+import os\\n+import subprocess\\n+import sys\\n+\\n+REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\\n+FROZEN_SAWC = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"sawc.py\\\")\\n+BUILD_DIR = os.path.join(REPO, \\\".build\\\")\\n+SAWC2 = os.path.join(BUILD_DIR, \\\"sawc2\\\")\\n+DRIVER_ENTRY = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"compiler\\\", \\\"driver\\\", \\\"src\\\", \\\"main.saw\\\")\\n+\\n+# (package name, directory relative to the repo root). Each stage is a package\\n+# the others import as `<name>.src.<module>`.\\n+STAGE_PACKAGES = (\\n+    (\\\"sawlex\\\", \\\"compiler/lex\\\"),\\n+)\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_program(entry, out):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compile `entry` to the executable `out`. Returns (ok, compiler output).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(out), exist_ok=True)\\n+    argv = [sys.executable, FROZEN_SAWC, entry, \\\"-o\\\", out]\\n+    for name, rel in STAGE_PACKAGES:\\n+        argv += [\\\"--module-path\\\", \\\"%s=%s\\\" % (name, os.path.join(REPO, rel))]\\n+    r = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=REPO, capture_output=True, text=True)\\n+    return r.returncode == 0, (r.stdout + r.stderr).strip()\\n+\\n+\\n+def build_sawc2():\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Build the driver to `.build/sawc2`. Returns (ok, compiler output).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return build_program(DRIVER_ENTRY, SAWC2)\\n+\\n+\\n+def main():\\n+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])\\n+    ap.add_argument(\\\"entry\\\", nargs=\\\"?\\\", help=\\\"a program to build instead of sawc2\\\")\\n+    ap.add_argument(\\\"-o\\\", dest=\\\"out\\\", help=\\\"the output path (required with ENTRY)\\\")\\n+    args = ap.parse_args()\\n+    if args.entry is None:\\n+        ok, output = build_sawc2()\\n+        target = SAWC2\\n+    else:\\n+        if args.out is None:\\n+            ap.error(\\\"-o is required when an ENTRY is given\\\")\\n+        ok, output = build_program(os.path.abspath(args.entry), os.path.abspath(args.out))\\n+        target = args.out\\n+    if output:\\n+        print(output)\\n+    if not ok:\\n+        print(\\\"build: FAILED: %s\\\" % target)\\n+        return 1\\n+    print(\\\"build: %s\\\" % os.path.relpath(target, REPO))\\n+    return 0\\n+\\n+\\n+if __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n+    sys.exit(main())\\ndiff --git a/compiler/tools/subset_check.py b/compiler/tools/subset_check.py\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..038ca1c6\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/compiler/tools/subset_check.py\\n@@ -0,0 +1,1629 @@\\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"The subset checker: refuse, in the compiler's own source, the shapes Stage 0\\n+mishandles.\\n+\\n+Stage 0 is the frozen Python compiler building the self-hosted one, and it is\\n+trusted only on the subset SL:architecture §4 defines. Each rule in `RULES`\\n+refuses one shape from §4's blanket list or one SL:hazards entry whose Checker\\n+line says yes; the diagnostic names the file, the line, the rule and its source.\\n+The source is parsed with the frozen compiler's own lexer and parser, so what is\\n+checked is exactly what Stage 0 reads.\\n+\\n+    python compiler/tools/subset_check.py           # the compiler tree\\n+    python compiler/tools/subset_check.py FILE...   # each file as a unit program\\n+\\n+The tree is every `compiler/**/*.saw` except `compiler/tests/**` (fixtures hold\\n+arbitrary Saw) and `*.test.saw` (sidecars, which only Stage 1 builds).\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+import dataclasses\\n+import glob\\n+import os\\n+import re\\n+import sys\\n+\\n+REPO = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))\\n+sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\"))\\n+sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\\n+\\n+import ast_nodes as A  # noqa: E402\\n+from lexer import Lexer, TokenType as T  # noqa: E402\\n+from parser import Parser  # noqa: E402\\n+from build import STAGE_PACKAGES  # noqa: E402\\n+\\n+ARCH = \\\"SL:architecture §4\\\"\\n+\\n+# rule name -> the document entry it enforces.\\n+RULES = {\\n+    \\\"lex\\\": ARCH + \\\": the frozen lexer must accept the source\\\",\\n+    \\\"parse\\\": ARCH + \\\": the frozen parser must accept the source\\\",\\n+    \\\"file-end\\\": \\\"SL:hazards C2\\\",\\n+    \\\"sync-only\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"closure-capture\\\": ARCH,\\n+    \\\"type-alias\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S13\\\",\\n+    \\\"argument-labels\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S14\\\",\\n+    \\\"index-receiver-call\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S15\\\",\\n+    \\\"cast-then-optional\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S17\\\",\\n+    \\\"program-unique-names\\\": \\\"SL:hazards S18\\\",\\n+    \\\"written-type-shape\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S19\\\",\\n+    \\\"float-literal\\\": ARCH + \\\"; SL:hazards S20\\\",\\n+    \\\"from-raw-literal\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L1\\\",\\n+    \\\"closure-syntax\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L2\\\",\\n+    \\\"default-value-literal\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L3\\\",\\n+    \\\"optional-shaping\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L4\\\",\\n+    \\\"empty-struct\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L5\\\",\\n+    \\\"bounded-extension\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L6\\\",\\n+    \\\"name-collision\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L9\\\",\\n+    \\\"type-param-receiver\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L10\\\",\\n+    \\\"leading-minus\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L12\\\",\\n+    \\\"nesting-depth\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L14\\\",\\n+    \\\"chain-length\\\": \\\"SL:hazards L14\\\",\\n+    \\\"interpolation-content\\\": \\\"SL:hazards C3\\\",\\n+    \\\"std-api\\\": ARCH + \\\": the API allowlist\\\",\\n+}\\n+\\n+# The std modules the compiler may import. S19).\\n+MAX_TYPE_DEPTH = 7\\n+\\n+STATEMENT_KEYWORDS = (T.RETURN, T.BREAK, T.CONTINUE, T.LET, T.VAR, T.GUARD, T.WHILE, T.FOR)\\n+# A `*` after one of these is multiplication (or, after a `.`, an import glob);\\n+# anywhere else it is a dereference.\\n+NOT_DEREF_BEFORE = (T.IDENT, T.INT, T.FLOAT, T.STRING, T.INTERP_STRING, T.RPAREN,\\n+                    T.RBRACKET, T.RBRACE, T.SELF, T.TRUE,  return \\\"%s:%d: %s: %s (%s)\\\" % (self.path, self.line, self.rule, self.message,\\n+                                       RULES[self.rule])\\n+\\n+\\n+class SourceFile:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"One file: its text, the frozen lexer's tokens and the frozen parser's tree.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, path):\\n+        self.path = path\\n+        self.rel = os.path.relpath(path, REPO)\\n+        with open(path, encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as fh:\\n+            self.text = fh.read()\\n+        self.tokens = None\\n+        self.program = None\\n+        self.lex_error = None\\n+        self.parse_error = None\\n+        lexer = Lexer(self.text)\\n+        try:\\n+            self.tokens = lexer.tokenize()\\n+        except Exception as exc:  # the frozen lexer raises more than text.split(\\\"\\\\n\\\")[0])\\n+\\n+\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+# Facts about std and the builtins, which share the program with the (type, method) -> {\\\"static\\\", \\\"instance\\\"}\\n+        std_dir = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"std\\\")\\n+        paths = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(std_dir, \\\"**\\\", \\\"*.saw\\\"), recursive=True))\\n+        paths.append(os.path.join(REPO, \\\"sawc\\\", \\\"builtin.saw\\\"))\\n+        for path in paths:\\n+            src = SourceFile(path)\\n+            if src.program is None:\\n+                raise RuntimeError(\\\"subset_check: cannot parse %s\\\" % src.rel)\\n+            prog = src.program\\n+            if path.endswith(\\\"builtin.saw\\\"):\\n+                gated = False\\n+                builtin = True\\n+            else:\\n+                leaf = os.path.relpath(path, std_dir)[:-len(\\\".saw\\\")].replace(os.sep, \\\".\\\")\\n+ decl in prog.structs + prog.enums + prog.traits + prog.type_definitions:\\n+                self.types.add(decl.name)\\n+                public = decl.visibility != A.Visibility.PRIVATE\\n+                if builtin or (public and not gated):\\n+                    self.prelude_types.add(decl.name)\\n+            for s in prog.structs:\\n+                if s.is_unsafe:\\n+                    self.unsafe_types.add(s.name)\\n+            for decl in prog.structs + prog.enums:\\n+                if any(tp.default is not None for tp in decl.type_params):\\n+                    self.defaulted_generics[decl.name] = len(decl.type_params)\\n+            for ext in prog.extensions:\\n+                for m in ext.methods:\\n+                    if m.type_params or ext.type_params:\\n+                        self.generic_methods.add(m.name)\\n+                    if m.is_borrows and m.name != \\\"[]\\\":\\n+                        self.borrows_methods.add(m.name)\\n+                    if not m.is_init:\\n+                        kind = \\\"static\\\" if m.is_static else \\\"instance\\\"\\n+                        self.method_kinds.setdefault((ext.struct_name, m.name), set()).add(kind)\\n+\\n+\\n+_STD = None\\n+\\n+\\n+def std_facts():\\n+    global _STD\\n+    if _STD is None:\\n+        _STD = StdFacts()\\n+    return _STD\\n+\\n+\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+# Small AST helpers.\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+def children(node):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The structural child nodes of `node`, in field order.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    out = []\\n+    for f in A.structural_fields(node):\\n+        _collect(getattr(node, f.name), out)\\n+    return out\\n+\\n+\\n+def _collect(value, out):\\n+    if isinstance(value, A.SawType) or value is type):\\n+        out.append(value)\\n+\\n+\\n+def walk(node):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`node` and every structural descendant, depth first.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    stack = [node]\\n+    while stack:\\n+        n = stack.pop()\\n+        yield n\\n+        stack.extend(reversed(children(n)))\\n+\\n+\\n+def type_parts(t):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"`t` and every type written inside it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    stack = stack.extend(cur.element_types or ())\\n+        stack.extend(cur.param_types or ())\\n+        stack.extend((cur.inner_type, cur.array_element_type, cur.func_return_type))\\n+\\n+\\n+def is_optional(t):\\n+    return t is not None and (t.kind == A.TypeKind.OPTIONAL or\\n+                              (t.kind == A.TypeKind.STRUCT and t.struct_name == \\\"Optional\\\"))\\n+\\n+\\n+def optional_payload(t):\\n+    if t.kind == A.TypeKind.OPTIONAL:\\n+        return t.inner_type\\n+    return (t.type_args or [None])[0]\\n+\\n+\\n+def root_name(expr):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The binding a place expression is rooted in, or None.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    while True:\\n+        if isinstance(expr, A.Identifier):\\n+            return expr.name\\n+        if isinstance(expr, A.SelfExpr):\\n+ elif isinstance(expr, A.ArrayIndex):\\n+            expr = expr.array_expr\\n+        elif isinstance(expr, A.TupleIndex):\\n+            expr = expr.tuple_expr\\n+        elif isinstance(expr, (A.ForceUnwrap, A.ReferenceExpr, A.OptionalChain)):\\n+            expr = expr.expr\\n+        elif isinstance(expr, A.MethodCall):\\n+            expr = expr.object\\n+        else:\\n+            return None\\n+\\n+\\n+def mentions(expr, name):\\n+    for n in walk(expr):\\n+        if isinstance(n, A.Identifier) and n.name == name:\\n+            return True\\n+        if isinstance(n, A.SelfExpr) and name == \\\"self\\\":\\n+            return True\\n+        if isinstance(n, A.MoveExpr) and n.variable == name:\\n+            return True\\n+        if isinstance(n, A.FunctionCall) and n.name == name:\\n+            return True\\n+    return False\\n+\\n+\\n+def pattern_names(pattern, fallback=()):\\n+    if pattern is None:\\n+        return [name for name in fallback if name and name != \\\"_\\\"]\\n+    names = []\\n+    for n in walk(pattern):\\n+        if isinstance(n, A.BindingPattern) and n.name and n.name != \\\"_\\\":\\n+            names.append(n.name)\\n+    return names\\n+\\n+\\n+def is_literal(expr):\\n+    if isinstance(expr, (A.IntLiteral, A.FloatLiteral, A.BoolLiteral, A.StringLiteral,\\n+                         A.NoneLiteral)):\\n+        return True\\n+    return (isinstance(expr, A.UnaryOp) and expr.op == \\\"-\\\"\\n+            and isinstance(expr.operand, (A.IntLiteral, A.FloatLiteral)))\\n+\\n+\\n+def is_collection_literal(expr):\\n+    return isinstance(expr, (A.ArrayLiteral, A.MapLiteral, A.SetLiteral))\\n+\\n+\\n+def arguments_of(call):\\n+    if isinstance(call, (A.FunctionCall, A.MethodCall, A.EnumInit)):\\n+        return [(a.name, a.value) for a in call.arguments]\\n+    if isinstance(call, A.StructInit):\\n+        return list(call.field_inits)\\n+    return []\\n+\\n+\\n+POSTFIX = (A.MethodCall, A.MemberAccess, A.ArrayIndex, A.TupleIndex, A.ForceUnwrap,\\n+           A.OptionalChain, A.CastExpr)\\n+\\n+\\n+def postfix_object(expr):\\n+    if isinstance(expr, (A.MethodCall, A.MemberAccess)):\\n+        return expr.object\\n+    if isinstance(expr, A.ArrayIndex):\\n+        return expr.array_expr\\n+    if isinstance(expr, A.TupleIndex):\\n+        return expr.tuple_expr\\n+    if isinstance(expr, (A.ForceUnwrap, A.OptionalChain, A.CastExpr)):\\n+        return expr.expr\\n+    return None\\n+\\n+\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+# The per-file checker.\\n+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+class BuildFacts:\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"What one build's own files declare: the names a call may resolve to\\n+    without reaching std, and the written types of struct fields and function\\n+    returns, for tracing an expression's type.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+    def __init__(self, files):\\n+ check_move_and_try(self, exprs, line):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"A `move` and a propagating `try` in one statement's expression.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        has_move = has_try = False\\n+        stack = list(exprs)\\n+        while stack:\\n+            n = stack.pop()\\n+            if n is None or isinstance(n, A.ClosureExpr):\\n+                continue\\n+            if isinstance(n, A.MoveExpr):\\n+                has_move = True\\n+            if isinstance(n, A.TryExpr) and n.variant == \\\"propagate\\\":\\n+                has_try = True\\n+            if isinstance(n, A.Block):\\n+                stack.append(n.final_expr)\\n+                continue\\n+            stack.extend(children(n))\\n+        if has_move and has_try:\\n+            self.report(line, \\\"try-ownership\\\",\\n+                        \\\"hoist each `try` into its own `let` before an expression that \\\"\\n+                        \\\"moves a value\\\")\\n+\\n+    # -- expressions -----------------------------------------------------------\\n+\\n+    def any(self, node, parent):\\n+        if isinstance(node, A.Block):\\n+            self.block(node)\\n+        elif children(node):\\n+                self.any(child, node)\\n+\\n+    def expr(self, e, parent):\\n+        if e is None:\\n+            return\\n+        if isinstance(e, A.Identifier):\\n+            self.use(e.name, e.line)\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.SelfExpr):\\n+            if self.closure_depth > 0:\\n+                self.report(e.line, \\\"closure-capture\\\", \\\"a closure may not capture `self`\\\")\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.MoveExpr):\\n+            self.use(e.variable, e.line)\\n+            b = self.lookup(e.variable)\\n+            if b is not None and b.kind == by \\\"\\n+                            \\\"`&` instead of moving it\\\" % e.variable)\\n+            if self.catch_depth > 0:\\n+                self.report(e.line, \\\"try-ownership\\\",\\n+ self.pop()\\n+            self.block(e.else_branch)\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.TryExpr):\\n+            if e.catch_block is not None and id(e) not in self.bound_try:\\n+ None`\\\")\\n+            self.expr(e.left, e)\\n+            self.expr(e.right, e)\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.ReferenceExpr):\\n+            if e.mutable:\\n+ in sawc/std and a \\\"\\n+                                \\\"copy in the new std\\\")\\n+            self.expr(e.expr, e)\\n+        elif isinstance(e, A.ArrayLiteral):\\n+            if root = os.path.join(REPO, \\\"compiler\\\")\\n+    out = []\\n+    for path in glob.glob(os.path.join(root, \\\"**\\\", \\\"*.saw\\\"), recursive=True):\\n+        rel = os.path.relpath(path, 100644\\nindex 35387986..00000000\\n--- a/selfhost/lexer/README.md\\n+++ /dev/null\\n@@ -1,173 +0,0 @@\\n-# sawlex — the Saw lexer, in Saw\\n-\\n-The first permanent module of the eventual stage1 (self-hosting) Saw compiler,\\n-and a measurement instrument for the rewrite decision (design 116). It is a\\n-faithful port of `sawc/lexer.py`: same token kinds, same token boundaries, same\\n-1-based `line:col` positions, and the same lex-error positions.\\n-\\n-The Python lexer's **observable output** is the correctness reference;\\n-`LANGUAGE_SPEC.md`'s lexical section is authoritative where the two disagree.\\n-\\n-## Layout\\n-\\n-```\\n-selfhost/lexer/\\n-  Saw.toml          # Blade package manifest (name = \\\"sawlex\\\")\\n-  src/lib.saw       # token model (TokenKind + Token + StringSegment) + `lex` + dump format\\n-  src/main.saw      # the `sawlex` CLI\\n-  tests/*.saw       # blade unit tests, one per token family\\n-```\\n-\\n-Build / run directly with sawc:\\n-\\n-```\\n-.venv/bin/python sawc/sawc.py selfhost/lexer/src/main.saw -o .build/sawlex\\n-.build/sawlex <file.saw>\\n-```\\n-\\n-or via Blade (`blade build` / `blade test`) like the `libs/` packages.\\n-\\n-## Canonical token-dump format\\n-\\n-`sawlex <file.saw>` emits one record per token, newline-separated:\\n-\\n-```\\n-KIND<TAB>line:col<TAB>escaped-text[<TAB>suffix]\\n-```\\n-\\n-* `KIND` — the token kind name (see the mapping table below); identical to the\\n-  Python lexer's `TokenType.name`.\\n-* `line:col` — 1-based, at the token's first byte/character.\\n-* `escaped-text` — the token's canonical text (decoded for strings; underscores\\n-  stripped and the base prefix lowercased for numbers), escaped at the **byte**\\n-  level: `\\\\\\\\` for backslash, `\\\\n` `\\\\t` `\\\\r` for those controls, `\\\\0` for NUL, any\\n-  other control byte (`< 0x20` or `0x7F`) as `\\\\xHH`, and every other byte —\\n-  including raw UTF-8 `>= 0x80` — verbatim.\\n-* `suffix` — a 4th column, present **only** for a fixed-width-suffixed integer\\n-  literal: one of `i8`/`i16`/`i32`/`i64`/`u8`/`u16`/`u32`/`u64`. `255u8` dumps as\\n-  `INT<TAB>1:1<TAB>255<TAB>u8`; every other token stops at the escaped text.\\n-\\n-### An `INTERP_STRING` record carries its segments\\n-\\n-An interpolated string's `escaped-text` is the literal's **raw source spelling**\\n-between the quotes (undecoded), and the record then continues with **one field\\n-per typed segment** (design 268), in source order:\\n-\\n-```\\n-INTERP_STRING<TAB>line:col<TAB>raw<TAB>T:text<TAB>E:line:col:raw-expr...\\n-```\\n-\\n-* `T:` — a literal run, **decoded** (so an escaped brace is a plain `{`/`}`).\\n-* `E:line:col:` — an interpolation, carrying the **raw** text between its braces\\n-  and the exact 1-based source position of its opening `{`.\\n-\\n-An empty `T:` run is never emitted, so two adjacent interpolations produce two\\n-consecutive `E:` fields. No token carries both a suffix and segments, so the\\n-tail is unambiguous. The segments are what a parser consumes: nothing is encoded\\n-into the token's bytes, which is what design 268 replaced (an escaped brace used\\n-to be the two bytes `0x01` + brace — indistinguishable from that same pair\\n-written as content, which silently lost it; SL-238).\\n-\\n-On a lex error the CLI emits a single record and exits nonzero:\\n-\\n-```\\n-ERROR<TAB>line:col<TAB>message\\n-```\\n-\\n-Error **positions and kinds** match the Python lexer; message **prose does not**\\n-(the differential harness compares the `ERROR` tag + position only).\\n-\\n-`tools/dump_tokens.py` emits the byte-identical format from the Python lexer;\\n-`tools/lexdiff.py` (`make lexdiff`) diffs the two dumps over every tracked `.saw`\\n-file. Zero mismatches over the corpus is the acceptance bar.\\n-\\n-## Doc-comment trivia dump\\n-\\n-`sawlex --docs <file.saw>` emits the documentation comments instead of the token\\n-stream, one record per captured line:\\n-\\n-```\\n-DOC<TAB>line:col<TAB>kind<TAB>escaped-text\\n-```\\n-\\n-* `kind` — `doc` for a `///` line, `module` for a `//!` line.\\n-* `line:col` — 1-based, at the leading `/`.\\n-* `escaped-text` — the line body with the `///`/`//!` marker and one following\\n-  space stripped, escaped by the same byte-level scheme as a token's text.\\n-\\n-Doc comments are **trivia**: `lex` skips them exactly as it skips `//` comments,\\n-so the token dump above is unaffected. `lex` returns one `LexResult` carrying\\n-the tokens, the doc records and the string-segment arena together — there is no\\n-tokens-only entry point, because a token's `seg_start`/`seg_count` index into\\n-that arena. Only a comment that starts its line is a doc comment; `////`\\n-(four or more slashes) and a `///` trailing code on the same line are ordinary\\n-comments.\\n-\\n-`tools/dump_tokens.py --docs` emits the same records from the Python lexer, and\\n-`make lexdiff` sweeps both dumps over the corpus (`--mode tokens|docs` picks one).\\n-\\n-## Kind-name mapping (TokenKind case → dump name)\\n-\\n-The Saw `TokenKind` enum is the model. Case names are Saw-idiomatic; the dump\\n-name column is what appears in a record and matches Python's `TokenType.name`.\\n-\\n-| TokenKind case | dump name | TokenKind case | dump name |\\n-|---|---|---|---|\\n-| `IntLit` | `INT` | `WrapAdd` | `WRAP_ADD` |\\n-| `FloatLit` | `FLOAT` | `WrapSub` | `WRAP_SUB` |\\n-| `StringLit` | `STRING` | `WrapMul` | `WRAP_MUL` |\\n-| `InterpString` | `INTERP_STRING` | `Not_` | `NOT` |\\n-| `BoolLit` | `BOOL` | `Move_` | `MOVE` |\\n-| `Ident` | `IDENT` | `Unsafe_` | `UNSAFE` |\\n-| `Func` | `FUNC` | `Assign` | `ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Let` | `LET` | `PlusAssign` | `PLUS_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Var` | `VAR` | `MinusAssign` | `MINUS_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `If` | `IF` | `StarAssign` | `STAR_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Else` | `ELSE` | `SlashAssign` | `SLASH_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Guard` | `GUARD` | `PercentAssign` | `PERCENT_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Return` | `RETURN` | `AmpAssign` | `AMP_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `True_` | `TRUE` | `PipeAssign` | `PIPE_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `False_` | `FALSE` | `CaretAssign` | `CARET_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Struct` | `STRUCT` | `ShlAssign` | `SHL_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `Extension` | `EXTENSION` | `ShrAssign` | `SHR_ASSIGN` |\\n-| `SelfKw` | `SELF` | `Question` | `QUESTION` |\\n-| `Init` | `INIT` | `DoubleQuestion` | `DOUBLE_QUESTION` |\\n-| `NoneKw` | `NONE` | `Exclaim` | `EXCLAIM` |\\n-| `Enum` | `ENUM` | `QuestionDot` | `QUESTION_DOT` |\\n-| `Case` | `CASE` | `DotDot` | `DOTDOT` |\\n-| `Match` | `MATCH` | `DotDotEq` | `DOTDOT_EQ` |\\n-| `While` | `WHILE` | `Ellipsis` | `ELLIPSIS` |\\n-| `Break` | `BREAK` | `LParen` | `LPAREN` |\\n-| `Continue` | `CONTINUE` | `RParen` | `RPAREN` |\\n-| `Trait` | `TRAIT` | `LBrace` | `LBRACE` |\\n-| `For` | `FOR` | `RBrace` | `RBRACE` |\\n-| `In` | `IN` | `LBracket` | `LBRACKET` |\\n-| `TypeKw` | `TYPE` | `RBracket` | `RBRACKET` |\\n-| `Extern` | `EXTERN` | `Comma` | `COMMA` |\\n-| `As` | `AS` | `Colon` | `COLON` |\\n-| `Try` | `TRY` | `Semicolon` | `SEMICOLON` |\\n-| `Catch` | `CATCH` | `Arrow` | `ARROW` |\\n-| `Static` | `STATIC` | `Dot` | `DOT` |\\n-| `Module` | `MODULE` | `At` | `AT` |\\n-| `Import` | `IMPORT` | `HashDirective` | `HASH_DIRECTIVE` |\\n-| `Public` | `PUBLIC` | `DollarParam` | `DOLLAR_PARAM` |\\n-| `Export` | `EXPORT` | `Newline` | `NEWLINE` |\\n-| `Package` | `PACKAGE` | `Eof` | `EOF` |\\n-| `Parent` | `PARENT` | `Plus` … `Tilde` | `PLUS` … `TILDE` |\\n-\\n-The remaining single-character operators/keywords map by uppercasing:\\n-`Minus`→`MINUS`, `Star`→`STAR`, `Slash`→`SLASH`, `Percent`→`PERCENT`,\\n-`Eq`→`EQ`, `Neq`→`NEQ`, `Lt`→`LT`, `Gt`→`GT`, `Lte`→`LTE`, `Gte`→`GTE`,\\n-`And`→`AND`, `Or`→`OR`, `Ampersand`→`AMPERSAND`, `Pipe`→`PIPE`,\\n-`Caret`→`CARET`, `Tilde`→`TILDE`. `Module`/`Import`/`Export`/`Package`/`Parent`\\n-exist in the enum for completeness _first_hit(paths, prefixes):\\n+    for p in sorted(paths):\\n+        for prefix in prefixes:\\n+            if p == prefix or (prefix.endswith(\\\"/\\\") and p.startswith(prefix)):\\n+                return p\\n+    return None\\n+\\n+\\n+def main():\\n+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])\\n+    source = ap.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\\n+
